# Contract - odradek

This contract is the executable spec for odradek. Every obligation below is stated so it can fail. The full contract is the first build act of this repo and it extends the stub obligations written here.

## Stub obligations

ZH-S1. Every suspect on the hunt list carries the evidence that it serves nobody, and a suspect listed without evidence is a defect the tests must catch.

ZH-S2. No kill fires before its proof is recorded, and an action taken on a suspect whose proof is missing or failed is a defect the tests must catch.

ZH-S3. The first action on any proven zombie is a reversible quarantine, and an irreversible action as the first step on any suspect is forbidden by the contract.

ZH-S4. Every hunt works from recorded or synthetic cluster state during the first slices, and no code path in those slices connects to a live cluster.

ZH-S5. Every fixture and example in this repo is synthetic and built for this repo, and no real trace or session data appears anywhere in the tree.

## Round 1 obligations

Round 1 works over one recorded cluster snapshot. A meaningful line is a line that is neither blank nor a comment, and a comment line is a line whose first character that is not a space is the hash character. A snapshot opens with one header line of the form snapshot followed by the snapshot time as a whole number of seconds on the snapshot clock. After the header come entry blocks in any order. An entry block opens with the word entry followed by the entry identifier as one token, and then carries exactly one line for each of these fields, in any order after the opening line. The kind field names process or run. The starter field names what started the entry as one token. The state field names running or finished. The signal interval field names a positive whole number of seconds. The last signal field names a whole number of seconds on the snapshot clock or the word none when no heartbeat or progress signal is recorded. The holds field names a comma separated list of resource tokens with no spaces or the word none when the entry holds nothing. The owner field names the identifier of the entry that owns this one or the word none when no owner is recorded. The token in-flight inside a holds list is the in-flight marker. Times are whole seconds on one stated clock so that every age the scan computes is an integer subtraction and no date parsing stands between the recording and the verdict.

Three proof rules decide every verdict, and each rule returns exactly one of satisfied, contradicted, or unknown for each entry. The signal rule is satisfied when the age of the last signal, snapshot time minus last signal, is at least three times the entry signal interval, contradicted when a signal is recorded and the age is under that threshold, and unknown when no signal is recorded. The owner rule is satisfied when the owner reference resolves to an entry recorded finished or to an identifier no entry in the snapshot carries, contradicted when it resolves to an entry recorded running, and unknown when no owner reference is recorded. The in-flight rule is satisfied when the holds list carries no in-flight marker and contradicted when it carries one, and it is never unknown because the holds field is always recorded. An entry recorded finished is settled and takes no verdict. A running entry whose signal age is under one signal interval is live. A running entry that satisfies all three proof rules is a proven zombie. Every other running entry is a suspect, and no suspect is ever reported as a zombie.

The scan prints a counts line naming the live, suspect, proven, and settled counts, then one block for every proven zombie and every suspect, sorted first by classification with proven zombies before suspects and then by identifier. Every block carries exactly three evidence lines, one per proof rule, a satisfied rule printed as an evidence for line, a contradicted rule printed as an evidence against line, and an unknown rule printed as a missing line, each line naming the recorded fact the verdict rests on. Resource tokens in a holds list print sorted so the output does not depend on the order the recording wrote them in.

The plan command writes a quarantine plan for one proven zombie and nothing else. The plan names the entry, restates the three evidence for lines of its proof, lists its steps with every step marked reversible, states an observation window of ten times the entry signal interval in seconds, states the escalation condition as a kill proposal drafted in a later round if the entry shows no signal for the whole window and no owner comes forward, and states that the tool executes nothing. Asking for a plan for an identifier the snapshot does not carry, or for an entry classified live, suspect, or settled, is refused with the identifier and the classification named.

ZH-1. A snapshot that breaks the recorded format is refused with an error that names the source and the defect, and the refusal covers a missing or duplicated header, an entry block before the header, an unknown field line, a duplicated or missing field inside a block, a duplicated entry identifier, a non positive signal interval, a last signal later than the snapshot time, and an unknown kind or state token, and no refusal ever surfaces as a traceback or as a partial document.

ZH-2. The signal rule is satisfied only when the recorded signal age is at least three times the entry signal interval, and an entry whose age falls one second short of that threshold is not satisfied, and an entry with no recorded signal returns unknown for this rule and can never be a proven zombie on the strength of the other two rules.

ZH-3. The owner rule is satisfied when the owner reference resolves to a finished entry or to an identifier absent from the snapshot, contradicted when it resolves to a running entry, and unknown when no owner reference is recorded, and an entry whose owner is recorded running is never a proven zombie.

ZH-4. The in-flight rule is contradicted when the recorded holds list carries the in-flight marker token and satisfied otherwise, and an entry holding the marker is never a proven zombie however stale its signal and however settled its owner.

ZH-5. A running entry with a signal age under one signal interval is classified live and is never listed, a running entry satisfying all three proof rules is classified proven zombie, every other running entry is classified suspect, and an entry recorded finished is classified settled and is never listed, and the classification of every entry is decided by these rules and no others.

ZH-6. The scan output lists every suspect and every proven zombie exactly once, sorted with proven zombies before suspects and by identifier inside each class, and it carries a counts line whose four counts equal the numbers of entries in each classification, and a snapshot with no suspects and no proven zombies prints the counts line alone.

ZH-7. Every listed entry carries exactly three evidence lines, one for each proof rule, with a satisfied rule printed as evidence for, a contradicted rule printed as evidence against, and an unknown rule printed as missing, and each evidence line names the recorded fact behind the verdict, so a listed entry without evidence cannot be printed.

ZH-8. The scan and the plan are pure functions of the snapshot text, so repeated runs over the same snapshot produce byte identical output, and shuffling the entry blocks or the tokens inside a holds list changes nothing in either document.

ZH-9. A plan asked for an identifier the snapshot does not carry is refused naming the identifier and its absence, and a plan asked for an entry classified live, suspect, or settled is refused naming the identifier and its classification, and each refusal is one error line with exit code 2 and no document on the output stream.

ZH-10. The plan for a proven zombie names the entry, restates the three evidence for lines of its proof, marks every step reversible, states the observation window as ten times the entry signal interval, states the escalation condition as a later round drafting a kill proposal, and states that the tool executes nothing, and a plan missing any of those parts is a defect.

ZH-11. The command line tool exits with code 0 when the scan or the plan document is produced and with code 2 when any input is refused, and a refusal prints one error line to the error stream and never a traceback, and the tool writes nothing to the standard output but the document it was asked for.

ZH-12. The tool reads only the snapshot file it is handed and prints documents, and no code path in this round connects to a live cluster, opens a network connection, or changes any input file, and producing a plan never performs the quarantine it describes.

ZH-13. The scan document opens with a header line naming the snapshot time it was computed from, written as the word snapshot followed by the recorded time, and the counts line and the entry blocks follow it in the order ZH-6 names.

## Round 2 obligations

Round 2 adopts the kill call the roadmap names, as an offline seam inside the plan and nowhere else. The scan never consults a judgment source. The plan consults one only for the proven zombie it names, only after the refusals of ZH-9 have had their say, and only from recorded fixtures in this round. A judgment answer can record a verdict on the plan and can advance the plan for operator approval, and it can do nothing else, and advancing a plan performs no part of it and approves nothing by itself.

ZH-14. The kill call reads one request built from the completed proof pack of one proven zombie and from nothing else. The request carries exactly four fields. The entry field names the entry identifier. The signal field, the owner field, and the in-flight field each carry exactly two fields, an outcome field naming that proof rule outcome for the entry as one of satisfied, contradicted, or unknown, and an evidence field naming that proof rule fact for the entry, copied word for word from the verdict the scan computed. The request is built by one builder from the verdict alone, so the same snapshot always builds the same request. A request naming a live entry, a suspect, a settled entry, or an absent identifier is never built, because the plan refuses those cases before any request exists.

ZH-15. A kill call judgment is recorded as one fixture pairing one request with one response. The fixture envelope carries exactly the fields synthetic, judgment_point, request, and response, the judgment_point field names the kill-call point, and the synthetic field is true, and a fixture carrying any other top level field or missing one of these is refused at load with the defect named. A response of the choice kind carries exactly the fields kind, choice, confidence, and probabilities, and a response of the abstention kind carries exactly the field kind and nothing else. The choice field names one of the three labels quarantine, watch, and spare. The probabilities field carries a number for each of the three labels and for no other label, each number between 0 and 1, and the three numbers add up to 1 within a tolerance of one thousandth. The confidence field is a number between 0 and 1. A response kind that is not a string is a malformed answer, and a numeric field whose value cannot be converted to a finite float is not a number for these checks, and an answer breaking any part of this shape is refused at load when it arrives in a fixture and counts as no judgment when it arrives from any other source.

ZH-16. The fixture player loads one directory of fixture files and validates every pair as it loads, and two fixtures recording the same request refuse the load, and a match is exact on the canonical JSON of the request, and where no fixture records the request the player abstains and returns no answer. The player reads only the directory it is handed and never reaches the network. A fixtures directory that cannot be loaded, because it is missing or unreadable or holds a fixture outside the shape ZH-15 states, refuses the plan command with one error line and exit code 2 and no traceback and no document on the output stream.

ZH-17. A judgment answer is trusted only when it is well formed for the kill call under ZH-15 and its confidence reaches 0.6, and an answer at exactly 0.6 is trusted and an answer under 0.6 is not. Before any answer is trusted it passes the same shape check the fixture player applies at load, whoever handed the answer back. An abstention, an answer under the threshold, an answer outside the shape, and no source at all each count as no judgment, and where no judgment stands the plan is the plan Round 1 wrote.

ZH-18. The plan command accepts a fixtures option naming a fixtures directory, and the directory is loaded before the plan is written, so a directory that cannot be loaded refuses the command on the terms ZH-16 states. The kill call is asked only inside the plan, only about the proven zombie the plan names, and only after the plan refusals of ZH-9, and a refused plan asks nothing. Where a trusted judgment stands, the plan records the verdict as two lines after the escalation line and before the closing line that states the tool executes nothing. The first verdict line is the words kill call verdict, then the label the judgment chose, then the words with confidence, then the confidence printed in the shortest decimal form that round trips to the recorded value. The second verdict line states the consequence. For a quarantine verdict the consequence line reads verdict advances this plan, the plan is ready for an operator to approve, and the approval itself stays an operator decision the tool never takes. For a watch verdict or a spare verdict the consequence line reads verdict leaves this plan unadvanced, no action follows from this answer, and the plan stands as written for an operator to read. Where no trusted judgment stands, the printed plan is byte identical to the plan Round 1 wrote for the same snapshot and identifier. A verdict never changes the classification line, the proof lines, the steps, the observation window, or the escalation line, and a plan carrying a verdict still closes by stating that the tool executes nothing.

ZH-19. The scan never consults a judgment source, and the scan document is byte identical whether the seam is present or not, and a plan written without the fixtures option never consults a judgment source either. No code path in this round connects to a live cluster or reaches a judgment provider, and every judgment the tool uses in this round comes from a recorded fixture in a directory it was handed.

## Round 3 obligations

Round 3 settles the readings of the holds list that the earlier rounds left unnamed, and it makes the one behaviour change the Round 3 probes forced. The probes run before this round was written found that a holds list carrying one token twice printed that token twice, once in the evidence lines and once in step 2 of the plan, because the parser kept the recorded tokens as written and the printers sorted them without deduplicating them. The list was sorted, as ZH-8 requires, and it was never a set. This round reads the list as a set at parse time, so the scan, the plan, and the kill call request all see each token once from one place, and no other reading of the snapshot format changes.

ZH-20. A holds list is read as a set of tokens, and the parser records each token once however many times the list writes it. A token repeated inside one holds list, the in-flight marker included, is the same recording as the token written once, and the scan document, the plan document, and the kill call request built over such a snapshot are byte identical to the same documents built over the snapshot with each token written once. That identity is the one behaviour change of this round, and it is confined to recordings that repeat a token inside one holds list. The holds field names resources and not entries, so an entry whose holds list carries its own identifier records nothing remarkable, and that token is inert unless it is the in-flight marker. A running entry may name itself as its owner, and the owner reference then resolves to a running entry, itself, so the owner rule is contradicted for that entry, and the entry is at most a suspect and never a proven zombie on that ground.

## Round 4 obligations

Round 4 reads the outcome of the watch the plan sets. The plan holds the quarantine for the observation window and watches for any signal, and its escalation line names what may follow a silent window, and until this round nothing in the tool read whether the window passed in silence. The observation report is that reading. It is deterministic and it consults no judgment source, and it performs nothing, and the scan and the plan and their documents do not change in this round.

ZH-21. The observe command takes the snapshot a plan was written from, a second snapshot recorded later, and the entry identifier the plan names, and it writes one observation report and nothing else. An observation asked over a second snapshot whose recorded time is not later than the earlier snapshot time is refused naming both recorded times. An observation asked for an identifier the earlier snapshot does not carry is refused naming the identifier and its absence, and an observation asked for an entry classified live, suspect, or settled in the earlier snapshot is refused naming the identifier and its earlier classification. Each refusal is one error line with exit code 2 and no document on the output stream and never a traceback.

ZH-22. The report opens with a line written as the words observation report for followed by the entry identifier, and then a line naming the earlier snapshot time after the words planned from snapshot, and a line naming the later snapshot time after the words observed at snapshot, and a line naming the elapsed seconds between the two times after the word elapsed and before the word seconds, and a line naming the observation window after the words observation window and before the word seconds. The window is ten times the signal interval the earlier entry records, computed from the earlier entry alone exactly as the plan computes it, so a signal interval the later snapshot records differently for the entry changes nothing in the report. Where the elapsed seconds fall short of the window the report gives the outcome line outcome watch still running and no verdict, and where the elapsed seconds reach the window the report gives exactly one verdict outcome line, outcome quarantine held or outcome quarantine broken.

ZH-23. The verdict reads the recorded facts of the later snapshot and nothing else. A sign of life is a last signal recorded in the later snapshot later than the last signal the earlier snapshot recorded, or an owner recorded running in the later snapshot under the owner rule the scan states. The quarantine broke when either sign of life stands, and it held when neither stands, and the entry absent from the later snapshot shows no sign of life. Any other recorded signal value, the same value, an earlier value, or no signal recorded at all, is no sign of life on the signal fact, and an owner in the later snapshot that is finished or absent or unrecorded is no sign of life on the owner fact. The verdict never reads the classification the scan gives the entry in the later snapshot, so an entry the later scan calls settled can still have broken the watch by signalling inside the window. The report states the evidence for its verdict as evidence lines in the printed style of the scan, two spaces in from the margin, a fact that supports the verdict printed as an evidence for line and a fact that broke the quarantine printed as an evidence against line, each line naming the recorded fact it read.

ZH-24. The observation rests on the proof that wrote the plan. A proven zombie satisfied all three proof rules in the earlier snapshot, so the earlier entry of a proven zombie always carries a recorded last signal, and the observe command reads that signal without refusing its absence. The report consults no judgment source and no fixture directory, and it is a pure function of the two snapshot texts, so repeated runs over the same pair of snapshots produce byte identical output. The report closes with the line executes nothing, this report is a document and the tool performs no part of it, and no code path in this round connects to a live cluster or changes any input file, and reading an observation never lifts or extends the quarantine it reports on.

## Round 5 obligations

Round 5 ports the landed behaviour of Rounds 1 to 4 to a native Rust crate and removes the Python tree in the same slice. The Python implementation at the Round 4 closure is the behavioural spec, and the port reproduces it exactly, documents, refusal lines, exit codes, and failure families included. The probes run before this round was written read the oracle's argument parser over full token sweeps, its path handling at the open and not only at the message, its dead stream behaviour on closed pipes, its fixture reader ceilings through real subprocesses, and its number conversions at their edges, and the obligations below state what those probes found wherever the reading differs from a sibling port or from a first guess.

ZH-25. The port is one Rust crate with a library target carrying the contracted behaviour and a binary target carrying the command line, and cargo tests are the primary suite. No Python file stands in the tree when the slice lands, and the tracked code of the slice is all Rust outside the remainder register ZH-34 names.

ZH-26. Only the help text and the malformed invocation error text follow clap. Every functional outcome matches the oracle exactly, the documents byte for byte, the refusal lines word for word, the exit codes, and the absence of any file creation, and an outcome whose oracle text is a traceback the port answers in its own voice at the oracle's exit code.

ZH-27. The argument scan walks the arguments the way the oracle parser's own scan walks them. Values group into segments ended by each flag the parser consumes, within a segment the first separator token is discounted and joins the share of the value just before it or the first share when it leads, each share gives up its first separator token, and a share not ending with exactly one token holds a list. A list standing in a slot surfaces in the uncaught family at exit 1 at the moment that slot is read, in the command's read order, so an earlier slot's ordinary refusal precedes a later slot's list and a plan's entry list surfaces only after the fixtures load. A trailing separator that closes a segment holding no positional, once a flag has been consumed, refuses the invocation as a usage outcome, and a trailing separator alone never vetoes a command that consumed no flag. Help fires where the oracle's help action fires and nowhere later than it, a pending flag value blocks help, long flags abbreviate unambiguously, and a flag token standing after a separator is a value and never a flag.

ZH-28. Every write the binary makes is checked. A document or help print whose failure is discovered while the document's encoded length is under 8192 bytes answers exit 120 with the port's own failed print line on the error stream, and one discovered at or past 8192 bytes answers exit 1 with the port's own uncaught line. An error line that cannot be written to the error stream answers exit 120 with no further output whatever family the line belonged to, and a successful run is unaffected by a dead error stream. The divide is pinned on closed pipes at 8191 and 8192 bytes of one scan document.

ZH-29. Every read opens the folded form of the path as written, where folding drops empty and single dot components, keeps parent references, keeps a doubled leading slash as the anchor where longer leading runs collapse to one slash, and reads an empty path as the single dot. The snapshot cannot read line and the not readable as UTF-8 line name the path as written, and this corrects the sibling reading under which the cannot read line named the folded form. A fixtures directory that cannot be loaded refuses naming the directory as written, an empty fixtures value loads the current directory, a fixture file that cannot be read or is not valid UTF-8 or is not valid JSON refuses naming the folded joined path of the directory as written and the child name, and a validation refusal and the duplicate fixture refusal name the child by its bare file name only. The child path is joined as the oracle's path join joins it, the directory as written and one separator and the child name, and no second separator stands where the directory already ends in one, and this corrects the join under which a root directory spelled with one slash named its children with two slashes and a root directory spelled with two slashes named them with one.

ZH-30. The fixture reader admits a document nested 9991 containers deep and raises the uncaught family on entering the 9992nd container, for the array carrier, the object carrier, and a mixed carrier alike. An integer literal of 4300 digits converts and one of 4301 digits raises the uncaught family, the sign not counted toward the limit, and a multi digit literal with a leading zero is a grammar refusal and never the limit. The literal negative zero converts to the integer zero, whose canonical text carries no sign. A grammar failure found while the reading stands inside 9988 or more containers raises the uncaught family rather than the load refusal, and this corrects the reading that a grammar failure at an admitted depth always refuses. That class holds the failures of the container's own reading, a delimiter or a key or a colon out of place and the input ending after a value, and the failures of the string reading, a string that does not close and a bad escape and a control character, in a value and in a key alike. A failure to find a value at all raises the uncaught family only inside the full 9991 containers and refuses in the ordinary way at any lesser depth, and that class holds a bad opening character, a literal that does not complete, the input ending where a value stands, and a number that does not begin, where a number that begins and then breaks off is a delimiter failure of the container's class instead. Where a fraction or exponent marker follows the integer part and no digit follows the marker, the token the reader matched is the integer alone and its conversion stands ahead of the delimiter failure the marker then causes, so an integer part past the limit raises the uncaught family ahead of that failure, and this corrects the reading under which the broken tail refused first.

ZH-31. A snapshot whole number is a run of characters in the Unicode decimal digit class and nothing else, converted digit by digit with each digit taking its value inside its own block of ten, so a run mixing scripts converts. The conversion carries the same 4300 limit counted over every digit character including leading zeros, and a longer run raises the uncaught family at exit 1. Admitted values are exact at any length, and every age, threshold, elapsed, and window computed from them is exact as a value, and leading zeros canonicalize away in every printed form. The print of a computed count carries the same 4300 digit limit as the conversion carries, and this corrects the reading that every computed count prints in full. A threshold is printed as its entry is classified, so an entry with a recorded signal whose interval makes the threshold pass 4300 digits raises the uncaught family at exit 1 in every command that classifies it, and an entry with no recorded signal prints no threshold and raises nothing. A window is printed by the plan and by the observation report after their refusals have had their say, so a proven entry whose interval makes the window pass 4300 digits raises the uncaught family at that print. No other computed count can pass the limit, because an age and an elapsed span are differences of admitted values and carry no more digits than the larger of the two values they differ.

ZH-32. The kill call trusts an answer only when its confidence reaches 0.6, an answer at exactly 0.6 trusted and one under it not, and the literal stands pinned beside its behavioural edges. The probabilities sum is the computed float sum taken in the label order quarantine, watch, spare, judged against a tolerance of one thousandth, with a computed distance from 1 strictly greater than the tolerance failing. The fixture match key is the canonical JSON of the point and request object with keys sorted, the oracle's default separators, and ASCII escaping with an astral character written as a surrogate pair. The verdict line prints the confidence as the shortest decimal form that round trips to its float value, and that form is the closest of the shortest forms to the exact value, an exact tie between two of them resolving to the one whose last digit is even, and this corrects the reading under which the last digit of an exact tie rounded up.

ZH-33. Every committed example regenerates byte for byte through the Rust binary, the scan, the plan in both its forms, and the observation report, and the committed fixtures load and answer as the oracle's player answers them. The argument and document differentials against the oracle stand in a test corpus with the per source counts pinned, oracle cases against clap presentation cases, so a case cannot silently change which side of the ZH-26 line it sits on.

ZH-34. The shipped crate depends on clap and thiserror and on nothing else outside the standard library, anyhow is confined to the binary entry, and no serde family crate appears in the shipped dependencies because the judgment seam carries its own JSON model and writer. Serde_json and sha2 stand as dev dependencies only, serde_json for the corpus reader in tests and sha2 for the template hash pin in tests, because no digest is product behaviour in this repo. Dependency placement pins read the manifest's dotted table forms as well as its plain sections and cover build dependencies, and hygiene scans resolve use trees in grouped, nested, aliased, and glob forms and bare module imports. The non Rust remainder of the tree is documentation and contract prose, the licence text, the manifest and its lockfile, the continuous integration file that only invokes the cargo gate, the gitignore, and the committed examples and fixtures in the data formats that are themselves the product interface, each read and asserted by Rust tests.

ZH-35. The setup invariants stand re-expressed as Rust tests, the stub obligations verbatim with their word counts, the canonical template prefix hash, the README round count pin reading exactly the one count 4 in this round, the dash scan over the text suffixes with the Rust suffix standing where the Python suffix stood, and the CI backstop step present and running the cargo gate.

## Round 6 obligations

Round 6 adds the estate form of the plan. A scan of a real estate leaves a set of proven zombies, and the operator plans for the set, so the plan command also takes the snapshot alone and prints one plan block for every proven zombie in it. The probes run before this round was written read the single plan document grammar line by line, the listed order of the scan and its bare counts line for an empty result, the reading the argument parser gives a plan call carrying one positional in every separator placement, the read order of the plan command from the snapshot read to the seam consultation, and the kill call request builder over the one verdict it is handed. The request builder reads that verdict and nothing else, so the estate form asks it once per proven zombie with no change to the request shape, and no request in the estate form carries any fact of the estate or of any other entry.

ZH-36. The plan command takes a snapshot and one entry identifier in its single form, and it takes the snapshot alone in its estate form, and the two forms are told apart by the number of positional shares the argument distribution assigns, one share assigning the estate form and two shares assigning the single form. The distribution is the distribution ZH-27 states, so a separator discounted into a share assigns that share in either form, a trailing separator that closes a segment holding no positional after a flag has been consumed refuses as a usage outcome in either form, and the fixtures option stands in either form wherever it may stand in the single form, standing or joined or abbreviated, with the last value winning where it repeats. A share holding a list in the snapshot slot surfaces at the snapshot read in the uncaught family, and a share holding a list in the entry slot surfaces only after the fixtures load, as ZH-27 states, and no third positional share stands in either form. This obligation qualifies ZH-9 and ZH-18 and the Round 1 plan paragraph on the stated inputs of the plan command, and their text stands, and on every other point they stand whole.

ZH-37. The estate plan opens with one line reading estate quarantine plan, and then it prints one plan block for every proven zombie in the snapshot, in the order the scan lists proven zombies, which is by identifier among the proven entries. Every block is byte for byte the document the single form prints for that entry, opening line, classification line, proof lines, steps, observation window, escalation line, any verdict lines, and closing line, and the blocks stand back to back with no separator between them. The estate document closes with the closing line of the single plan, the line stating that the tool executes nothing, standing once for the estate as a whole after the last block, and the document ends with one trailing newline.

ZH-38. An entry that is not a proven zombie contributes no block to the estate plan and is skipped silently, and no refusal of ZH-9 fires in the estate form, because no identifier is named and no entry is asked for. A snapshot with no proven zombies prints the opening line and the closing line and nothing else, at exit 0, and that empty estate is a valid document and never a refusal.

ZH-39. The estate form keeps the read order of the single form whole at the level of the snapshot. The snapshot read, the snapshot parse under ZH-1, the classification, and the fixtures load under ZH-16 each refuse on their own terms before any kill call question is asked, and a refusal at any of those steps prints no document and asks nothing. The estate builder then checks the observation window of every proven entry ahead of the first question, so a proven entry whose window passes the digit limit of ZH-31 raises the uncaught family with no question asked about any entry and no document printed.

ZH-40. Where a judgment source stands, the estate plan asks the kill call once for every proven zombie, in the order the blocks print, and each request is built by the one builder of ZH-14 from that entry verdict alone and carries exactly its four fields. A trusted answer marks its own entry block with the two verdict lines ZH-18 states and marks no other block, and an abstention, an answer under the threshold, an answer outside the shape, and an unmatched request each leave their block as the document the single form prints where no judgment stands. Where no judgment source stands, every block is the document of Round 1, and a question is never asked about an entry that is not a proven zombie.

ZH-41. The single form does not move. Its documents, its refusals, and its exit codes are byte identical to the rounds before this one everywhere, the committed examples and the committed fixtures included, and the scan and the observation report do not move at all. The one presentation change is the help and usage text of the plan command, where the entry identifier now stands as optional in the form the clap model prints, because the plan command takes the snapshot alone as well. ZH-14 and ZH-19 stand whole, so the scan never consults a judgment source and a plan in either form written without the fixtures option never consults one either.

ZH-42. The estate plan is a document and the tool performs no part of it. Every block states its steps reversible and closes by stating that the tool executes nothing, and the estate document closes with the same statement, and no code path in this round connects to a live cluster, changes any input file, or performs a quarantine the document describes, and the tool reads only the snapshot file and the fixtures directory it is handed.

ZH-43. One family of plan invocations changes its answer in this round beyond the presentation change ZH-41 names, and this obligation adjudicates it. An invocation carrying one positional share, where the share before this round could not fill the entry slot and the invocation refused as an under-full single form, is read under ZH-36 as the estate form, and it answers as the estate form answers. A fixtures pair standing in such an invocation now reaches the fixtures load of ZH-16, which fires or refuses ahead of the estate, where before this round the usage refusal fired first and the load was never reached. A token standing after a discounted separator in such an invocation reads as the estate snapshot, where before this round the invocation refused as a usage outcome. A share holding a list in such an invocation surfaces at the snapshot read in the uncaught family at exit 1, where before this round the invocation refused as a usage outcome at exit 2. ZH-41 stands qualified on this family alone, and its byte identity holds for every invocation that read as the single form before this round, and for the scan and the observation report whole.

## Round 7 obligations

Round 7 rebuilds the README to the fleet README standard and aligns the repo's one description on every surface it stands on, and it changes no behaviour. ZH-268. Five cases fire the surface. Every case stages the standing earlier snapshot of observe-whitespace-tab-separators with every tab of its text replaced by the round's point, the standing later snapshot of that case at its standing bytes, and answers the standing observation report of that case at exit 0. observe-whitespace-next-line-separator writes the next line point at every place the twin writes a tab. observe-whitespace-ogham-space-separator writes the ogham space mark. observe-whitespace-paragraph-separator writes the paragraph separator. observe-whitespace-narrow-no-break-separator writes the narrow no-break space. observe-whitespace-medium-math-space-separator writes the medium mathematical space. The token identity of every staged text was verified before any case was baked, replacing the round's point with a tab reproduces the twin text byte for byte and every line's field split agrees with the twin's, because the reader splits its lines on the line feed alone and the table holds all five points, so each staged text parses token for token as its tab twin and the observe command prints the twin's standing report. Every expectation was derived from the reader code before it was baked by execution against the binary built from this tree. The mechanics stand in the reader order and the controls of the round stand on them. The observe command reads its two snapshots through the one snapshot reader, and the reader splits the text into lines on the line feed alone, and each line is stripped over the whole whitespace table before it is weighed, so any point of the table standing at either end of a line is read off the line first. Each surviving line then splits into its fields over the same table, so any point of the table standing between two tokens separates them exactly as the tab does, and the header line and the entry line and the field lines are all weighed on their tokens alone. A point the table does not carry standing between two tokens leaves them joined as the one token they then are, and a point struck from the table at the end of a line rides into the last token of the line and changes that token's reading, and a point struck from the table between two tokens joins them into the one token they then are. The report the command prints is built from the parsed snapshots and never from the written forms of the lines, so two snapshot texts whose tokens agree print the one report however their whitespace is written. ZH-332. The census of this round is over the written digit forms of the staged snapshot texts of the standing corpus and over the whole number reader that reads them, and it follows the census of Round 69 to the families that census left standing. A census by mechanism over the 467 standing cases reads every staged text the cases carry and finds 547 snapshot texts and 175 fixture texts, the snapshot texts standing 147 behind the scan command, 190 behind the plan command, and 210 behind the observe command. Every whole number in the format reads through the one helper of the snapshot module, which admits a value whose every point stands in the decimal digit table the text module carries and converts it digit by digit at the value each point carries as its offset within its block of ten, mixed scripts accepted digit by digit, under the limit of 4300 digit characters a conversion admits. The table stands at 64 ranges and 680 points. The fixture reader is no consumer of the table, reading its numbers over the ASCII digits alone, and the argument classifier reads the table only for its negative number pattern, and no staged fixture text and no argument of any case carries a decimal point beyond the ASCII digits. Across the 547 snapshot texts the whole number readings number 7050 and carry 261569 digit characters, of which 260862 are ASCII digits and 707 are not. The 707 stand 227 behind the scan command, four of them in scan-digits-mixed-scripts and 40 in the seven cases of Round 58 and 61 in the ten cases of Round 61 and 61 in the ten cases of Round 64 and 61 in the ten cases of Round 67, and 240 behind the plan command, 60 in the nine cases of Round 59 and 60 in the nine cases of Round 62 and 60 in the nine cases of Round 65 and 60 in the nine cases of Round 68, and 240 behind the observe command, 60 in the nine cases of Round 60 and 60 in the nine cases of Round 63 and 60 in the nine cases of Round 66 and 60 in the nine cases of Round 69. The Arabic-Indic block of the table, the ten points that run from U+0660 to U+0669, therefore stands fired at every one of its points behind the scan command and at nine of its points behind the plan command and behind the observe command. The Extended Arabic-Indic block of the table, the ten points that run from U+06F0 to U+06F9, stands fired at every one of its points behind the scan command and at nine of its points behind the plan command and behind the observe command. The Nko block of the table, the ten points that run from U+07C0 to U+07C9, stands fired at every one of its points behind the scan command and at nine of its points behind the plan command and behind the observe command, and the block stands closed at every placement. The Devanagari block of the table, the ten points that run from U+0966 to U+096F, stands fired at every one of its points behind the scan command and at nine of its points behind the plan command and behind the observe command, and the block stands closed at every placement. Every other block of the table stands bare at every point, 630 points in 59 ranges, at every placement. The only other points beyond the ASCII range in the snapshot texts are the accented e of scan-listed-unicode-id and the Deseret capital letter of plan-fixtures-surrogate-pair-entry, each standing once in an entry identifier and in no number reading. Behind the scan command every one of the ten digit values stands in the number readings of a standing scan text, the digits of values zero, one, two, three, four, five, six, eight, and nine standing in the readings of the estate text scan-example-estate stages at 29, 2, 1, 3, 1, 3, 4, 2, and 15 times, and the digit of value seven standing once in the last signal of scan-signal-age-at-threshold, while behind the plan command and behind the observe command the digit of value seven stands in no number reading of any standing text, so a block stages at ten points behind the scan command and at nine points behind the plan command and behind the observe command. The surface of this round is the Bengali block of the table, the ten points that run from U+09E6 to U+09EF, staged behind the scan command, the block standing next in the table after the Devanagari block Rounds 67, 68, and 69 closed and the oldest digit family standing bare, taken at the placement where a block stages at its full count, on the pattern of one family at one placement a round the whitespace rounds set. The ten are the family's full count at this placement, because the census finds exactly ten points of the block stageable behind the scan command, so this round takes ten cases and not a case more or fewer. The leavings this census names are the digit of value seven of the Arabic-Indic block behind the plan command and behind the observe command, which stands in no reading there and no twin stages, and the digit of value seven of the Extended Arabic-Indic block behind the plan command and behind the observe command likewise, and the digit of value seven of the Nko block behind the observe command likewise, and the digit of value seven of the Devanagari block behind the plan command and behind the observe command likewise, the Bengali block behind the plan command and behind the observe command, where it stages at nine points, and the remaining 58 ranges, 620 points, standing bare at every placement.

ZH-333. Ten cases fire the surface. Every case stages a standing snapshot text with every occurrence of one ASCII digit value in its number readings written as the Bengali digit of the same value and answers the standing report of its twin at exit 0. scan-digits-bengali-zero stages the estate text of scan-example-estate with the digit zero of its readings so written, the point standing 29 times in those readings. scan-digits-bengali-one writes the digit one, standing twice. scan-digits-bengali-two writes the digit two, standing once. scan-digits-bengali-three writes the digit three, standing three times. scan-digits-bengali-four writes the digit four, standing once. scan-digits-bengali-five writes the digit five, standing three times. scan-digits-bengali-six writes the digit six, standing four times. scan-digits-bengali-seven stages the snapshot text of scan-signal-age-at-threshold with the digit seven of its last signal so written, the point standing once in that reading. scan-digits-bengali-eight writes the digit eight, standing twice. scan-digits-bengali-nine writes the digit nine, standing 15 times. The token identity of every staged text was verified before any case was baked, returning the round's point to its ASCII digit reproduces the twin text byte for byte and every number reading of the staged text carries the same digit values as the corresponding reading of its twin, because the whole number helper converts a reading digit by digit at the offset each point carries within its block, so a reading written in the digits of the Bengali block parses to the value of the same reading written in ASCII digits, and no identifier and no other byte of either twin text is touched, the points standing in the number readings alone. The derivation rule is the scan digit derivation rule Round 61 settled, reproduced byte for byte on the ten standing scan digit cases of Round 67 before any case of this round was derived. Every expectation was derived from the reader code before it was baked by execution against the binary built from this tree. The mechanics stand in the reader order and the controls of the round stand on them. The snapshot header names the snapshot time as a whole number, and the signal interval and the last signal of every entry read as whole numbers through the one helper, and the helper refuses a value carrying any point outside the decimal table and converts a value inside it digit by digit, the digits of two scripts in one reading converting side by side at their own offsets. The digit limit counts digit characters over every script alike, leading zeros included, so a reading past 4300 digit characters in any block stands past the limit as an ASCII reading does. The reports the commands print are built from the parsed values and never from the written forms of the readings, so two snapshot texts whose readings carry the same values print the one report however their digits are written. The standing corpus of 467 cases passes whole against the build of this tree in a run whose summary line carries the full count, and the enlarged corpus of 477 cases passes whole against the same build in a run whose summary line carries the full count, and the ten new cases stand baked by that second run and not by derivation alone.

ZH-334 states the mutations of the round and the sets they failed, each set taken from a run of the whole enlarged corpus whose summary line carries the full case count. The arms strike the points of the Bengali block from the decimal table from the top of the block downward, because the value of a digit is its offset within its range, so a strike that took a lower point and left a higher point standing in a range beginning above its block would misread the higher point's value, while a strike from the top leaves every surviving point in a range whose start is unchanged and every surviving reading at its value. The predicted set of every arm was computed by mechanism from the enlarged corpus before any arm ran, a case standing in the set of a strike exactly when a number reading of one of its staged snapshot texts carries a Bengali digit at or above the struck point, and every executed set matched its prediction. Each arm is an exact text substitution on the one range line of the decimal table in the printable table module, narrowing that range or striking it, and each arm was built by that substitution, with the module source restored to its standing bytes after every arm and the restore verified by SHA-256. Ten cases of the round fail under an executed arm, so the pins discriminate the ten digits of the Bengali block as decimal digits of the whole number reading behind the scan command, each digit now pinned at the first placement of the block.
